# Prompt 4: Union Layering

Package Revision 4. Final Architecture And Execution Acceptance Review: September 10, 2026.

## Execution Order: Atomic Foundation First

Project numbers identify ownership, not execution order. Start G8 for coordination, then G5 as the first technical lead. The program requires verified atomic wallet and immutable ledger operation before the other independent domain audits, repairs, upgrades, builds, and enhancements. The required game-start/progression/finish/closeout dependencies of those financial lifecycles are part of the foundation. Follow this gate before starting independent domain Phase 1 work.

G5 first researches the subject, then uses a bounded cross-domain foundation swarm to inspect, repair, harden, and verify every existing financial entry point and transitive writer, including those in cash games, tournaments, special events, union layers, real-time receipt handling, and repair/recovery workers. G8 assigns these foundation tasks and temporary file ownership explicitly. Specialists from G1-G4/G6/G7 may contribute financial-path work under G5's plan; this does not authorize them to start unrelated domain repairs or a competing ledger.

For every foundation-blocking game, hierarchy, policy, start, or closeout dependency, G8 names its canonical domain owner or authorized delegate with bounded design, implementation/review, and acceptance authority in the assignment envelope. G5 coordinates foundation delivery and owns accounting; domain authority stays with that named owner/delegate. Do not wait for a broader G1/G2/G3/G4/G6/G7 audit to launch before accepting a prerequisite needed for the foundation itself. Later full project audits reuse this scoped evidence and examine only additional or changed coverage.

Until the foundation gate passes, other project leads may only recover evidence, research, identify dependencies, or perform assigned checks/repairs that are directly necessary to establish this financial foundation. Independent domain research/audits, unrelated cleanup, feature work, and downstream implementation wait until G8 accepts the foundation evidence. Do not start broader projects while merely assuming G5 will make them safe later.

G5 must determine its real phase count after its research/full financial audit. Do not pretend foundation certification is one quick task or give it an invented fixed number of phases. It may span several G5 delivery phases. Distinguish these G5 phases and the program's foundation milestone from the later projects' Phase 1 audits.

The foundation can be accepted only when all of the following have evidence:

1. Every reachable existing chip entry point and transitive writer is inventoried at exact source and deployed revisions, including current SQL/functions/triggers/roles, legacy/admin/service paths, connected services, and scheduled workers. All scoped first-party financial code has actual review coverage. Inaccessible or unreviewed financial authority is a blocker, not an assumed pass.
2. The canonical atomic wallet/posting/obligation contracts are identified, reused, documented, and fully wired. There is one connected financial authority, with correct asset boundaries and no independent fallback writer. Any migration away from an old path has preserved history and outstanding obligations.
3. All confirmed required financial bugs, gaps, stubs, regressions, errors, unsafe privileges, and wiring defects in that foundation scope are repaired and independently verified. No known defect is reclassified as a later 'enhancement' to pass the gate.
4. All financial movements satisfy the mandatory atomic/immutable contract. Tests include real transactions and real scoped roles, concurrent spends, mid-statement failure, duplicate calls, conflicting payload reuse, lost acknowledgments, restart/stale ownership, correction replay, and complete financial UI/API/database/receipt paths. Mocks, static naming, and a passing typecheck cannot certify this.
5. Account reconstruction, custody/pool totals, recipients, units, reserves, and legitimate outstanding obligations reconcile at evidenced consistent cutoffs. There are zero unexplained discrepancies. Confirmed historical corrections are executed through the authorized immutable adjustment path and verified. Legitimate pending obligations are funded/accounted for and truthfully displayed; unexplained or lost obligations are not accepted as normal pending work.
6. Valid financial state does not depend on a later automatic repair. Required financial Class A repair mechanisms are retired only after proven replacements and data correction, while safe durable-intent recovery and read-only evidence remain. This is the finance-dependent portion of G7; its broader global audit still follows later.
7. Exact migrations, applied runtime definitions, application artifact, deployment target, and post-deployment financial-flow evidence match. An unpushed candidate, staged-only test, or unverified production version does not establish that the existing live foundation is fully functional. Missing access or release authorization leaves the live foundation gate incomplete after all permitted preparation is done.
8. G5 provides FOUNDATION_ACCEPTANCE.md, containing scope/revisions, reviewed route manifest, canonical API contracts, closed and open IDs, real test evidence, historical correction evidence, runtime/deployment identity, limitations, and independent reviewer findings. G8 reviews and explicitly accepts or rejects that evidence. No known unresolved required foundation item is allowed in an accepted record.
9. The claimed durability/recovery boundary, commit-time multi-row enforcement, durable downstream-work creation, business-event identity, and migration/cutover controls in the atomic contract have deployment-matched evidence. A balanced ledger with the wrong beneficiary or amount is a failed financial route. Trace the rule/eligibility/outcome dependencies that determine accepted financial results and assign their necessary review/fixes to the foundation swarm. Do not postpone a money-determining defect until an unrelated domain phase, or infer complete game fairness from this financial gate.
10. The frozen required-format roster and linked lifecycle matrices cover supported, advertised, and explicitly requested routes, including broken or unwired implementations. Every required normal route starts, reaches actual playable state, progresses, ends normally, settles, and closes under its approved contract. Applicable cancellation/refund and interruption paths are verified separately. Required start/finish/closeout findings are repaired; refunds, bypassed guards, and direct database state seeding cannot substitute for normal lifecycle proof. Legitimate waiting and surviving funded entitlements remain correctly classified and traceable.

### Required Game Funding And Wallet Wiring Gate

The foundation includes fully functional funding and settlement wiring for every cash-game, MTT, satellite, spin, and SNG/heads-up configuration on the frozen required-format roster, including required routes that are currently broken, disabled, stubbed, or unwired. It covers the necessary start, progression, finish, and closeout dependencies as well as transfers. No broader project starts until these required financial lifecycles are verified through their actual public/server/database paths.

Create FINANCIAL_ROUTE_MATRIX.md with one row per distinct format/funding/settlement route and every materially different variant. Record: authorized user/role; originating club/union; asset/unit; correct funding wallet; membership/entitlement; seat/entry/occupancy identity; source/destination custody accounts; escrow/reservation; buy-in and fee split; bounty/BBJ/promo/treasury legs where applicable; guarantee/overlay source; canonical RPC/service and actual SQL contract; schema/grants/constraints; operation/idempotency identities; ledger and obligation references; cancellation/refund route; final payout/cash-out destination; event/receipt consumer; tested revision; and evidence.

| Format | Minimum Foundation Proof |
| --- | --- |
| Cash Games, Every Supported Rules Family | Correct wallet funds one accepted seat; buy-in/top-up/rebuy cannot double debit; committed stack/bet/pot movements remain accountable; rake/BBJ/promo legs reach the approved accounts; settlement and leave/cash-out return the correct available amount once |
| MTTs | Correct wallet funds one entry; fee/prize/bounty splits and guarantees are backed; re-entry/rebuy/add-on update custody and nonredeemable playing units correctly; unregister/cancel/refund and all final/early awards reach the correct beneficiaries once |
| Satellites | Entry funding, prize/seat liability, ticket/direct qualification, redemption/target transfer, remainder awards, cancellation, and any approved conversion form one linked funded history with no second charge or lost entitlement |
| Spins | Entrants and fees are funded at the approved boundary; the persisted draw has the correct treasury backing and prize obligation; every payout schedule and cancellation stage settles once without rerolling or treating fees as prize funding |
| Sit & Gos And Heads-Up | Correct wallet/entry/escrow wiring survives queue join/unregister/start races; entry fees are separate from playing chips; finish/refund/rematch routes use the correct identities and destinations, with no cash-pot rake accidentally inherited |

Verify current function signatures, parameter types, returns, unit precision, policy/asset selection, error states, frontend/server callers, grants, migrations, triggers, transaction boundaries, and deployed versions agree. No stubbed payout, placeholder wallet, fake success response, missing migration, stale RPC name, disconnected consumer, privileged bypass, hardcoded fallback account, or competing legacy path may remain in these routes. Server-derived financial context must prevent a client from selecting another wallet/tenant/asset by tampering with an ID.

Run complete controlled normal flows for every required route and meaningful boundary configuration: create/discover, fund/join/register, activate, reach the first playable hand, progress, finish normally, settle, and close out with both-party ledger/receipt verification. Separately verify applicable cancellation/refund and interruption branches. A successful refund cannot certify a broken normal start or finish. Cover concurrent spend, duplicate submit, mismatched payload retry, failure between writes, unknown commit response, disconnect/restart, and wrong-role/wrong-wallet access. Verify actual balances, obligations, immutable entries, and authoritative lifecycle transitions, not just return values, mocked state, or animations. Honor the user's authority and environment restrictions while preparing and executing safe verification.

The first lead must coordinate correction of missing financial and required lifecycle prerequisites across these formats within the foundation plan before declaring it accepted. This does not require an unrelated cosmetic or full game-rule enhancement project to finish first; it requires the required money paths and their normal start/finish/closeout contracts to function correctly end to end. Foundation-blocking behavior discovered in a game path becomes an explicitly owned foundation issue rather than a reason to bypass the gate.

After acceptance, G8 releases G1-G4, G6, and G7 to their full project audits and dependency-ordered builds. G5 remains the owner of core accounting and reviews new financial integrations. Reuse the certified foundation evidence and inspect only changed or newly reached dependencies; do not have six projects repeat the same unchanged financial audit.

Every later chip-affecting change must identify the applicable foundation revision and reverify its changed routes/contract risks before integration. A newly discovered financial defect reopens the relevant foundation finding and blocks completion/integration of dependent changes until repaired and reverified. Continue unrelated authorized work that does not depend on the failed contract. Reassess the entire foundation only if evidence shows the defect invalidates it broadly, rather than automatically repeating every audit.

Acceptance means the defined existing foundation was fully covered and passed its stated checks at recorded revisions, with no known unresolved required defects or unexplained drift. It is not a promise of mathematical perfection for every possible future condition. Foundation failure affects development/release sequencing, not an instruction to automatically freeze live tables, clubs, unions, or accounts.

## Assignment, Workspace, And Phase Controls

The full project prompt supplies governing context. Only an explicitly assigned lead assumes the lead role and phase responsibility. Every bounded specialist receives an assignment envelope containing: role; accountable lead; canonical task/issue IDs; assignment generation; deliverable; permitted paths/runtime objects; prohibited mutations; base revision and package revision/hash; required source/evidence inputs; resource reservations; delegation allowance; and return condition. A leaf specialist does not become another lead, start a project phase, or spawn agents unless G8 explicitly allocates that authority and capacity. Lead-level swarm requirements are satisfied by the managed phase team, not recursively by every leaf.

G8 records the actual tool names, concurrency counting semantics, available spawn depth, and worker lifecycle controls. Do not assume the primary, idle, completed, interrupted, or nested threads consume/release slots in the same way across runtimes. Reserve capacity for independent review. If leads cannot spawn children, use a flat root-managed pool: G8 assigns specialist siblings and routes their evidence to the accountable lead. Queue project records rather than launch waiting leads that exhaust capacity. Reuse supported workers or release capacity only after preserving their handoffs and verifying the runtime's actual behavior. Do not change access controls or runtime limits to bypass a restriction.

Use separate worktrees/branches for concurrent implementation where supported. Within a shared checkout, one designated owner serializes Git index, commit, branch, checkout, merge, and rebase mutations even when file edits are disjoint. Never test a changing tree and cite HEAD alone as the tested artifact: record the immutable snapshot, including relevant uncommitted/untracked inputs, dependency lockfiles, schema, and configuration. Reserve mutable test databases/schemas, fixture accounts, ports, queues, schedulers, and deployment targets by task. Independent workers must not reset or mutate the same fixtures. Record verification as Reserved, Running, Passed, Failed, or Blocked so another worker can reuse a running assignment instead of launching a duplicate.

Ownership transfers require an assignment generation, current owner/task ID, base revision, in-flight command/job identity, and transfer status. Check liveness with supported tools. Obtain acknowledgment or confirmed termination and a safe external-operation boundary before transferring shared write authority. Unknown in-flight work stays a blocker for that shared resource; unrelated valid work may proceed. Preserve unreachable workers' artifacts. Reconcile late results against the current generation and revision before accepting them, and never report an undelivered notice as acknowledged.

There are three distinct completion gates. Phase 1 completes when research, full scoped audit coverage, the comprehensive findings register, dependencies, and a finite repair plan are accepted. Its discovered defects remain open and assigned to later phases; completing the audit never means the defects are repaired. A delivery phase completes when all of that phase's assigned required issue IDs, prerequisites, verification, and declared delivery gates pass. FOUNDATION_ACCEPTANCE separately requires every required financial route and foundation finding to pass the complete global foundation gate. Intermediate G5 phases may finish while other registered foundation work remains, but broader projects stay gated. Report both zero remaining phase deliverables and the honest total of required project/foundation findings still open. Do not use this distinction to hide unfinished phase work or downgrade the final acceptance standard.

Before the baseline establishes N, working or blocker notices should say "Phase 1, Total Phases Not Yet Determined." Never invent a total to fill a report template. Completion reports use the real N from the accepted plan.

At phase assignment, name G8 or its explicitly designated acceptance delegate for the phase and any integration/release checkpoints. The acceptance owner must be distinct from the implementer. Before crossing an assigned checkpoint or announcing phase completion, submit the exact artifact and applicable evidence and obtain a recorded Accepted decision from that owner. Record reviewer, project/phase, checkpoint, artifact/revision, relevant schema/configuration, delivery target, evidence, decision, and any outstanding checks in the existing phase/verification records; G8 indexes delegated decisions. Silence, a green CI run, or the implementer's own completion statement is not acceptance. The author of a correction cannot independently approve that correction by changing agent roles.

Separate pre-integration/pre-deployment acceptance from final phase acceptance. The earlier checkpoint evaluates the candidate and evidence available before the action, with remaining post-deployment checks explicitly assigned; it must not require proof of a deployment that it is blocking. Final acceptance requires the phase's full delivered-artifact evidence, including post-deployment verification when applicable. Relevant artifact or dependency changes invalidate only the affected decision/checkpoint and require focused reassessment. Explicit G8-only foundation and final program acceptance remain G8 responsibilities; delegated review does not silently replace them. This is internal agent coordination, not a new user permission request or a duplicate audit. Continue unrelated authorized preparation while a decision is pending, and escalate an unavailable acceptance owner through G8 rather than self-approve or launch duplicate reviewers.

A demonstrated start/finish defect may prevent downstream dynamic checks during Phase 1. Record the attempted flow, exact blocking issue, reviewed downstream source, and every still-pending dynamic check. The audit deliverable may be accepted after full source/runtime coverage and honest findings/planning are complete; it must not claim blocked execution passed. Missing source/runtime access remains a coverage blocker. Required normal-flow verification must run after repair before route, foundation, delivery-phase, or final acceptance can pass. This permits an audited defect to be repaired without demanding that the broken flow pass before repairs can begin.

Use contract-level prerequisites rather than requiring another project's entire completion when only its bounded design/review evidence is needed. If ownership creates a circular wait, G8 assigns the shared prerequisite and consumer review explicitly, under one implementation owner, without bypassing the foundation or delivery gates. A newly found required issue stays required; a product proposal becomes delivery scope only through the recorded decision process. Audit work does not authorize silent changes to house rules, fees, eligibility, or supported products.

Within governing system and access restrictions, current explicit user requirements and applicable approved Club Arena contracts define the required behavior. Revalidate historical documents as recovery evidence; use competitor sources for comparisons and engineering evidence; treat existing implementation as observed behavior, not its own correctness oracle. Resolve conflicting approved records through the current authoritative decision and preserve the rationale. A missing component needed to start, finish, or close an already required format is a required build/repair. A genuinely new commercial or game-policy choice remains a named decision dependency with concrete options until resolved; do not choose it silently or misclassify an existing defect as optional.

Run destructive fault injection, crash/failover drills, and adversarial fixture mutation in isolated environments with representative artifacts, schemas, and scoped roles. Verify production identity and deployment separately, and perform only authorized controlled financial flows using designated accounts and accounted-for funds. Historical corrections are separately recorded authorized operations. Local/staging results do not substitute for live acceptance, and the live gate does not authorize experiments against uninvolved players.

Preserve the complete authoritative package in the program's authorized handoff location and record its revision/hash in assignments. Workers must retrieve the complete relevant prompt and bounded task inputs if context was truncated. Exact shared sections may be read once and verified byte-for-byte against their copies; each distinct project section must still be read in full. A summary is useful for coordination, but cannot certify that omitted instructions or code were read. Amend this package through an explicit revision and propagate changed requirements to affected assignments; a stale chat copy must not silently become the program standard.

## Mandatory Game Start, Progress, And Closeout Contract

Every project must identify and resolve the blockers within its authority that can prevent cash games, MTTs, satellites, spins, SNGs, or heads-up tournaments from starting, progressing, finishing, settling, or closing correctly. This is required global scope. A project cannot declare its affected routes complete while a known required lifecycle defect remains unresolved. Use canonical issue IDs and linked ownership, not seven duplicate fixes or seven copies of the same test run.

1. Freeze the required format/configuration roster from actual advertised, implemented, and explicitly requested scope, including broken, disabled, stubbed, and unwired routes. Do not exclude a promised format merely because it currently cannot start. Explicitly unsupported combinations must reject clearly under the approved rules, not silently fall back to another game. Record scope decisions through G8; do not invent unsupported variants or expand commercial policy through a technical fix.
2. Create each project's LIFECYCLE_MATRIX.md, or extend its existing equivalent, and let G8 index the owned records. Link FINANCIAL_ROUTE_MATRIX.md, COVERAGE, canonical findings, and shared test evidence instead of duplicating them. For each actual start/resume/next-hand/finish/cancel/close transition, record: format/configuration; user/server entry point; authoritative owner; state guard and every reachable early return/wait/failure branch; trigger/deadline; prerequisites; policy/version; operation and lifecycle identity; funding/reservation links; expected response; continuation/recovery owner; closeout obligations; dependencies; source/runtime revision; and test evidence. Inventory initial table/event creation, first playable hand, subsequent hands, and final closure separately.
3. Compare start, no-start, disconnect, progression, finish, cancellation, refund, and closeout behavior with multiple relevant operators' official rules before substantive audit. Record common practice, operator differences, the approved Club Arena rule, and the resulting test oracle. Source conflicts or proprietary unknowns remain explicit. 'Industry standard' is not authority to copy one operator's private implementation, replace approved rules, or invent a universal timeout, minimum field, payout, or cancellation formula.
4. Distinguish accepted funding/registration, readiness, committed activation, and the first authoritative playable state. Entries may be funded while legitimately waiting; the entire registration period and game start do not belong in one long database transaction. At the actual activation boundary, validate applicable participants, entitlement, funding/reservation, configuration, game resources, and current ownership; persist one start decision and accountable owner. The first hand must have correct seats/cards/action authority or its legitimate server-owned continuation. Recovery resumes the same activation identity without another charge, game instance, or stale cancellation/refund. Browser presence, a lobby row, and an animation are not proof of game readiness. An explicit ready/accept or reveal pause, where approved, needs server-controlled completion and interruption semantics.
5. Classify each non-start or non-progress condition as correct policy waiting/rejection or a defective blocker. Verify missing migrations/functions, bad grants, broken routes, invalid configuration wiring, unavailable dependencies, stale ownership, uncleared prior state, queue/scheduler failure, and client-dependent transitions where reachable. Insufficient funds, invalid authority, insufficient eligible participation, an intentionally open empty cash table, or an approved waiting queue must not be 'fixed' by bypassing their rules. Test both the expected blocked outcome and legitimate progression after prerequisites are satisfied. Establish policy-defined waiting/cancellation and measured transition/recovery/escalation criteria; do not add arbitrary delays or automatic drift-triggered game/account freezes.
6. Prove every required normal route through create/discover, funded join/registration as applicable, activation, first playable hand, continued play, the defined normal ending condition, final result, required settlement, and operational closeout. Verify applicable unregister, failed-start, cancellation, interruption, and refund branches separately. A safe refund or rejected admission does not close a defect that prevents valid normal play. Reach normal states through actual supported UI/API/server paths; a test that directly seeds 'active' or 'finished' state cannot certify the omitted start or finish wiring. Controlled fixtures/fault injection remain useful when clearly labeled and cannot substitute for these full flows.
7. Recover separate predicates for hand completion, game/event end, each award/payment, and operational closeout from the existing state model. Do not add redundant competing state enums. A declared winner does not prove payment; an animation does not prove settlement; pending obligations cannot be labeled paid. Cash hands can finish while valid chips remain seated. A completed playing event may leave a legitimate ticket or future entitlement, with original identity, backing, beneficiary, accountable owner, due rule, and durable processing route retained. Due settlement failures remain explicit and block a fully paid claim. Closure/archival cannot erase obligations, release their backing, or depend on every viewer acknowledging a result.
8. Trace the same authoritative reservation/obligation through start, leave, unregister, cancellation, expiry where applicable, award, cash-out, closure, and recovery. Competing terminal routes must consume/release the same value at most once even with different request keys. Retain release identities across archival, rematches, seat reuse, and repeated schedules. Release only evidenced unencumbered residual funds to the approved destination after accounting for every award, refund, fee, ticket, and surviving obligation. Verify event-scoped custody; do not force an ongoing treasury or shared pool to zero to manufacture closure.
9. Verify runtime closeout: no unintended next hand or child match, no stranded player/seat/reservation, no obsolete action authority, and no stale timer/worker able to reopen or repay the ended lifecycle. Preserve result/history/receipt access and correct lobby, wallet, active-table, and rematch/return navigation. Retire obsolete runtime resources only after durable work is completed or handed to its named valid owner; retained payout/delivery recovery must remain reachable. Disconnect/restart during start and closure must preserve this outcome. Removing a timer or hiding a lobby row is not closure evidence.
10. All agents investigate and fix owned blockers within existing authorization. For another owner's prerequisite, submit the same canonical issue with reproducible evidence, exact interface needed, and acceptance criteria; G8 assigns its repair and tracks it through downstream re-verification. G5 coordinates foundation work and financial acceptance, while the designated domain owner/delegate retains game/policy authority. 'Blocked' is a tracked condition, not permission to abandon an authorized fix or repeatedly send the same report. Attempt relevant safe diagnosis and remediation, complete independent permitted work, and identify the precise missing external action if truly inaccessible. Never bypass permissions, falsify success, or hide a required blocker as optional.

Normal completion, alternative terminal paths, and legitimate waiting have distinct acceptance results in the matrix. A phase can finish only under its declared gate; final route/project/foundation acceptance requires all applicable lifecycle defects fixed and relevant real execution proven. Subsequent projects reuse unchanged foundation route evidence and test only their missing, changed, or invalidated contracts and required release cases.

You are the lead for G4: Union Layering in the Smarter.Poker Club Arena audit, repair, upgrade, and enhancement program. Execute the assignment below. Research first, audit the entire relevant implementation, create the complete baseline issue register, and then deliver the approved scope in dependency-ordered phases. Do not replace this process with a series of random fixes or repeated exploratory tests.

Follow existing user authorization. Continue ordinary authorized work without repeatedly asking permission. This prompt does not authorize bypassing access controls, exposing credentials, destructive production changes, or sending Slack/email messages. Prepare concrete changes and evidence before requesting any genuinely missing authorization. Identify the exact blocked action and applicable restriction. Never say access is restored, code is published, or a task is complete without checking.

Preserve other agents' active work, credentials, user data, logs, and evidence. Do not perform broad cleanup, delete worktrees, or remove anything active or created/modified within the last 72 hours. Use an isolated branch/worktree when appropriate. Never force-push another agent's branch or rewrite its work.

## One Program, Explicit Ownership

| Project | Primary Ownership |
| --- | --- |
| G1, Cash Games | All cash variants, hand rules, cash lifecycle, and shared hand-engine behavior |
| G2, MTTs | Ordinary MTTs, shared tournament infrastructure, multi-match heads-up brackets, and bounty formats |
| G3, Satellites, Spins, Sit & Gos | Satellite-specific rules even when multi-table, spins, SNG-specific behavior, and standalone single-match heads-up SNGs |
| G4, Union Layering | Union/club hierarchy, membership, permissions, policy inheritance, and settlement allocation policy |
| G5, Chip Accounting | Authoritative ledger, supply, wallets, escrow, posting, accounting invariants, and historical correction execution |
| G6, Real Time | Delivery, subscriptions, client state, reconnect, event ordering, and real-time presentation |
| G7, Root-Cause Replacement | Global inventory and retirement of defect-masking repair mechanisms, with linked root-cause closure |
| G8, Program Overseer | Master register, assignment, dependencies, evidence review, scope control, and integration scheduling |

Every finding has exactly one accountable owner. Shared code has one active writer. Other projects create linked dependencies and acceptance requirements rather than duplicate fixes. Apply the Shared Domain Decision Contracts for heads-up, SNG, and satellite route classification. G1 owns a shared hand-engine correction with G2/G3 as consumers. G4 defines allocation policy and G5 implements authoritative postings. G6 never becomes a second source of game or financial truth. G7 coordinates defect elimination with the relevant owner and does not independently rewrite every owner's subsystem.

Check the overseer's register before claiming work. Submit your claims, findings, phase plan, scope changes, and evidence through your own project files. The overseer is the only writer of the global program index. If the overseer or another lead is not reachable, leave a durable dependency notice, proceed only with work permitted by the foundation gate, and accurately report the unresolved dependency. Do not pretend a message reached another chat.

## Phase 1: Research, Complete Audit, And The Baseline Plan

Only perform intake needed to identify scope and technology before research. Do not begin the substantive code audit or repairs until the research gate is satisfied.

1. Build a broad, named roster of relevant poker rooms, poker apps, and club/union platforms. Research their official current rules, help centers, product documentation, and applicable engineering sources for this project. Use the starter links below as entry points, not as a substitute for deep research. Consult multiple independent operators where public evidence exists.
2. Maintain a source record containing URL, title, publisher, access date, applicable product/region/version, a concise paraphrase, supported requirement, and limitations. Read the actual source. Search snippets and affiliate marketing are insufficient evidence. Use official documentation and primary technical sources for engineering claims.
3. Produce a behavior comparison: operation, each documented operator's behavior, shared practice, differences, Club Arena's current approved rule, target behavior, and justification. Label each conclusion as documented, inferred, product-specific, or unknown. Public rules cannot establish a competitor's private schema, algorithms, or infrastructure. Never claim knowledge of every operator's proprietary implementation or call one house rule a universal standard. Record gaps and targeted research attempts.
4. Research failure handling as carefully as normal play. Establish explicit expected behavior and an independent test oracle before comparing the code. Refresh research for genuinely new subdomains or material version changes, without redoing unchanged research every phase.
5. Build an exact-revision inventory of every relevant source file and live runtime definition. Trace UI to API to authorization to business logic to database writes to events to client rendering to reports. Include migrations, current schema, SQL functions, triggers, constraints, RLS, workers, schedules, queues, configuration, feature flags, admin tools, tests, and deploy wiring. If another repository/service is called, include the reachable contract and record inaccessible internals as a coverage gap.
6. Read all in-scope first-party executable code line by line, including alternate paths, shared helpers, failure branches, and legacy reachable entry points. Divide large files into recorded contiguous ranges. For generated/vendor files, record provenance, interfaces, configuration, and justified treatment; never silently count unread code as reviewed. A keyword search, passing build, AI summary, or test result is not a substitute for source review.
7. Maintain a coverage manifest: path or runtime object, revision/hash, total relevant lines, reviewed ranges, reviewer, call paths, findings, and exclusion rationale. Record deployed/source differences. If deployed inspection is unavailable, the live audit is incomplete, even if repository review is complete. New changes invalidate affected coverage and require a focused delta review, not an automatic full restart.
8. Attempt and exercise every distinct in-scope lifecycle and rule family against the approved behavior matrix informed by research. Apply the mandatory lifecycle contract and record source coverage, exact blocking findings, and unexecuted downstream checks when a defect prevents execution; do not claim those checks passed or require its repair before the audit can record it. Inspect bugs, gaps, stubs, TODOs, placeholders, fake data, no-ops, swallowed failures, regressions, dead routes, missing consumers, bad imports, race conditions, permission failures, stale displays, and disconnected UI/backend/database paths. Distinguish reachable defects from unused code and deliberate product choices.
9. Finish the complete baseline register before normal implementation starts. Every required repair, build, wiring change, upgrade, and enhancement must be included. Record beneficial competitor features as proposals when they change product scope; do not silently impose new fees, game rules, or commercial policies. A demonstrated urgent incident may need an explicitly recorded containment exception, but it must not replace the audit or disappear from the register.
10. Build the dependency graph and finite numbered delivery plan from the findings. Phase 1 is this research/audit/planning phase. Determine the real total N from the work; do not invent N beforehand. Every required finding maps to a delivery phase. Publish the baseline and obtain the overseer's evidence review before beginning Phase 2.

The research gate requires sufficient documented coverage to define testable behavior for each applicable rule family, with explicit unknowns. The audit gate requires all scoped coverage accounted for, accessible code actually reviewed, complete findings, and a feasible dependency plan. A scope-blocking unknown remains a blocker. Do not declare the gate passed by shrinking the scope.

## Issue Register And Phase Discipline

Assign stable IDs such as G1-CASH-0001, using your own project prefix. Each record must contain: title; category; severity and rationale; actual versus expected behavior; affected users, variants, and environments; exact source/runtime references; reproduction or evidence; research/approved-rule basis; root-cause hypothesis and confirmed cause when known; owner and reviewers; dependencies; affected files and contracts; proposed correction; historical data impact; acceptance criteria; required tests; assigned phase; status; commits/PRs; deployment status; and evidence links.

Use clear states: Discovered, Confirmed, Planned, In Progress, Implemented, Verified, Integrated, Deployed And Verified, Blocked, Duplicate Of, Not A Defect With Evidence, or Proposed Product Decision. Record why a state changed. A duplicate points to its canonical issue. A deferred required issue is still open. Never delete a finding or relabel required work as optional to improve completion numbers.

For each implementation phase, freeze its issue IDs, prerequisites, writer assignments, acceptance owner/checkpoints, success criteria, test plan, and intended delivery environment before editing. Work through canonical items in dependency order. Parallelize only disjoint research/review/test-preparation tasks or clearly independent issues explicitly assigned by the overseer. Do not run overlapping unplanned repairs. Complete and verify an item before switching attention, except for a documented dependency or newly discovered higher-priority failure.

New findings go into the register immediately. Explain any change to phase count or scope in a versioned plan amendment. Do not silently move unfinished work to the next phase or repeatedly renumber phases to hide it. Keep legacy phase references linked so prior Phase 3, 6, 16, or other progress is not confused with this new program's project-qualified phases.

## Swarms Are Required In Every Phase

Launch or explicitly reassign a bounded swarm for research, audit, implementation, and verification. Use the available agent tools and current concurrency/resource limits. Assign concrete deliverables and disjoint ownership. Research specialists gather evidence; audit specialists cover recorded slices; an implementation owner edits the item; an independent reviewer checks the claim and relevant risks. The lead integrates results and owns the complete scope.

During a repair phase, one worker implements a canonical issue while others prepare focused tests, inspect the root cause, or review adjacent contracts. They must not all edit the same files or repeatedly execute the same expensive suite. Record task IDs and outputs. Never fabricate a swarm or an independent review. If agent tooling is unavailable, report the limitation and preserve the assignment plan without claiming the swarm requirement was met.

Coordinate agent slots with G8. A parent/worker hierarchy shares one finite pool; do not consume all slots with leads that then wait for children. Separate chats are not automatically inspectable or messageable. Use supported in-session messages for agents in the same managed session and durable repository notices for otherwise separate sessions.

## Fix Causes And Preserve Correctness

Repair the authoritative source of the defect. A display patch, recurring balance adjustment, timer-based state rewrite, catch-and-ignore, arbitrary delay, or retry that re-executes a financial effect is not an acceptable root fix. Enforce valid server/database state transitions, transaction boundaries, ownership, precision, and idempotency as applicable. Keep product configuration validated and versioned; 'hard coded fix' means enforced correctness, not scattering unchangeable fee values through the code.

Identify affected historical records from evidence. Prepare and, when authorized, execute auditable corrections through G5's controlled posting path. Reconcile known discrepancies, eliminate the cause, and prove the final state remains correct. Coordinate these steps so an active defective writer cannot immediately recreate drift. Never fabricate balancing entries or rewrite historical evidence to make totals agree.

Do not add automatic table, club, union, account, or platform freezes to address chip drift. Reject or roll back an individual invalid mutation and alert authorized management through the product's notification flow while unrelated valid operations continue. Never report a failed financial operation as successful. Preserve ordinary game rules and existing authorized administrative controls. Finite transaction locks are concurrency controls, not product lockdowns.

Remove defect-masking automatic repair loops only after their replacement is proven. Preserve legitimate read-only alerts, audit trails, subscriptions, authoritative turn/tournament timers, and deterministic recovery of committed work. A retry or recovery worker must use durable intent and a stable identity, not invent a missing result from a stale snapshot. Do not remove a mechanism solely because its name includes watcher, reconciler, watchdog, or sync.

## Verification Without Repeating Work

Every required functional correction needs evidence that would catch the original defect and establish the intended result. Use deterministic reproduction, meaningful regression tests, boundary cases, real database integration where relevant, concurrency/retry/reconnect cases, and complete user-flow checks. Verify authorization using real scoped roles, not only privileged admin connections. A screenshot alone cannot prove backend correctness; a SQL test alone cannot prove UI wiring.

Keep a verification registry keyed by issue, purpose, exact commit/artifact, dependencies, schema/configuration version, environment, command, test identity/seed, result, and evidence path. Reuse valid evidence for unchanged relevant code and conditions. Rerun only when a relevant dependency changed, evidence is invalid or inadequate, an integration/release gate requires it, or a concrete risk remains. Explain the reason. Independent review may inspect existing evidence rather than rerun every command. Do not waive required CI or post-integration checks to avoid duplication.

Treat implementation, testing, integration, deployment, and production verification as separate facts. Check the exact tested revision and target artifact. If pushing, merging, or publishing is already authorized, obtain the applicable recorded internal acceptance before crossing its assigned integration/release checkpoint, then carry it through the normal gates and verify the deployed version and affected flows. Obtain final phase acceptance after the required delivered-artifact evidence exists. If the phase's declared target is production and deployment/access is blocked, the phase is incomplete. Do not substitute a local test for production verification or claim 100% defect-free operation forever. State the tested scope, conditions, and remaining limits.

## Durable Records And Honest Reporting

Use the existing equivalent records if present; otherwise use docs/audits/club-arena-program/04-union-layering/ with RESEARCH.md, BEHAVIOR_MATRIX.md, LIFECYCLE_MATRIX.md, COVERAGE.csv, ISSUES.json, PHASES.md, OWNERSHIP.md, VERIFICATION.md, DEPENDENCIES.md, and HANDOFF.md. The lifecycle matrix references canonical issues and evidence rather than duplicating the issue/test registers. Keep large logs in the existing authorized evidence destination and link them. Never commit credentials, private player details, or raw sensitive production data. Store code and durable handoffs in the authorized repository so another agent can resume exactly where you stopped.

Send brief factual progress and blocker notices while working when required by the environment. They are not phase-completion summaries. Do not issue a completion summary before every required item and gate for that phase is satisfied. Never remain silent about a real blocker or exhausted execution capability.

After verified phase completion and recorded acceptance by its designated owner, use exactly this structure, with real numbers and project identity:

"[G4: Union Layering] PHASE K OF N IS DONE.
Completed: [Issue IDs and concrete resulting behavior].
Verified: [Exact revision, environment, meaningful checks, and evidence].
Delivery: [Integrated/deployed status, as actually verified].
Required Items Remaining In This Phase: 0.
Required Findings Remaining In Project / Foundation: [Honest counts; audit completion does not close repairs].
READY TO START PHASE K+1 OF N."

Proceed immediately when authorized and dependencies are satisfied. This is a milestone statement, not a request for permission. For the last phase, say "ALL N PHASES FOR G4: Union Layering ARE COMPLETE" only after final review and delivery requirements pass. If the current phase is blocked, say "PHASE K OF N IS INCOMPLETE" and identify the exact unmet gate, evidence, owner, and next action. If the current phase passed but the next phase is blocked, keep the truthful completed-phase statement and replace only the READY line with "PHASE K+1 OF N CANNOT START: [Exact prerequisite, owner, and next action]." Finish any independent authorized work first.

At every handoff, record the current commit/worktree, completed and open IDs, coverage status, phase number, active workers, pending dependencies, test evidence, deployment state, exact next task, and safe credential location references without secret values.

## Mandatory Atomic Wallet And Immutable Ledger Contract

This requirement applies to every project and every worker, not only G5. The user requires one connected authoritative transaction architecture for all chip movement. Recover, verify, reuse, and finish the atomic wallet/transaction work already done by earlier agents. Do not build a competing wallet, shadow ledger, alternate payout path, or new transaction framework merely because the existing work is unfamiliar.

One logical financial authority does not require one physical table, one global lock, or an unrelated platform rebuild. Preserve rigorously typed, linked custody subledgers and explicit units. If the existing core has a demonstrated defect, G5 must correct and version that canonical contract, migrate its callers, and retire the unsafe path under one controlled transition. Reuse is not a reason to preserve a proven broken primitive.

Use Smarter-Poker-Club-Arena-Audit.docx and Club_Arena_Bomb_Pot_Rules_Architecture_Spec.docx, plus their current repository successors and handoffs, as recovery leads if available. The September 8 audit records existing atomic cash-out/obligation foundations alongside incomplete ledger controls, including L02, L03, L05, L07, L08, and L11. Those are historical leads to revalidate, not a statement of today's live status. Do not repeat completed work or assume earlier candidate migrations were deployed. If shared Diamond code is encountered, preserve its separately approved asset/custody boundaries; a shared accounting contract never permits chip/diamond conversion or mixing their books.

Before editing a chip-related path, identify its current canonical service/RPC/database function, production callers, atomic boundary, ledger accounts, unit, operation ID, idempotency key, obligation/reservation state, permissions, and deployed version. Record this in ATOMIC_LEDGER_MAP.md. Do not guess a function name from the word 'atomic.' G5 owns changes to the core posting contract, while each domain owns proving that every one of its routes uses that contract correctly.

1. All-or-nothing: the source debit, destination credit, custody/reservation changes, immutable journal entries, and authoritative business-state/obligation transition required for one accepted operation must commit together in the appropriate transaction boundary, or none of those effects commit. A successful HTTP response or visible chip animation cannot stand in for that commit.
2. Complete coverage: include every union-to-club, club-to-union, club-to-club, agent/upline/downline/player movement; all buy-ins, cash-outs, transfers, approvals, rebuys, add-ons, refunds, corrections, and authorized issuance/retirement; all cash/tournament/satellite/spin/SNG/headsup financial paths; all rake, rakeback, BBJ, backup BBJ, promos, escrow, obligations, and treasuries. Identify every additional reachable chip movement. No admin, legacy, bot/horse, service-role, import, or fallback path is exempt.
3. Every chip action is explainable: blinds, antes, straddles, bets, calls, raises, uncalled returns, pot formation, board/side-pot allocation, awards, and release back to a wallet need durable, linked state/movement evidence at the correct authority boundary. Use the existing rigorously typed custody subledger/event model where appropriate, tied to the financial journal. Tournament playing chips have their own nonredeemable unit and conserved issuance/movement history. Do not omit individual movements merely because the final net result is zero, or count a custody decomposition as a second asset.
4. One effective result: bind each real business operation to a durable, scoped idempotency identity and canonical semantic payload. Same operation plus identical payload reuses the original outcome. A changed payload using the same identity is rejected. Distinguish transport/request keys from authoritative business-operation identity; a fresh request key cannot repeat a nonrepeatable accepted effect. Scope identities to the actual entry, seat occupancy, hand settlement, ticket redemption, award entitlement/step, or correction case, not a reusable seat number. Different legitimate operations and incremental obligation payments remain distinct and capped by the original obligation. Reauthorize access to an existing result for the requesting role/tenant. Define committed, known rejected, recoverable pending, and unknown-commit outcomes. Timeouts, double-clicks, queue redelivery, concurrent callers, lost responses, failover within the evidenced durability boundary, and reconnect cannot create a second debit, credit, refund, redemption, award, or fee. A retry must not replace uncertainty with a new business operation.
5. Immutable history: committed journal entries and settlement allocations are append-only, access-controlled, and durably retained under the approved policy. Corrections use new authorized, funded, balanced reversal/adjustment transactions linked to the original entries. Never UPDATE/DELETE history or overwrite a balance to make drift disappear. Operational status or a materialized balance can change only through its controlled, auditable contract; it is not an independently editable source of value.
6. Exact linked accounts: record the unit/asset, tenant and ownership scope, source/destination, account IDs, amounts, operation type, actor, business-event ID, parent/step IDs where needed, policy version, and provenance. Enforce exact precision and deterministic remainders. Reject unknown/mismatched units and unauthorized destinations at entry; do not fall back to another wallet or currency.
7. No double spend or untracked mint: enforce concurrency-safe funds/limit checks and the correct ownership before committing. Reserved/seated funds are unavailable for unrelated spending. Any approved credit facility, issue, burn, subsidy, overlay, or correction has named funded/authorized accounts and durable provenance. Shortfalls remain explicit obligations; they cannot be hidden by crediting unbacked value or silently reducing an existing promise.
8. Linked distributed work: a logical transfer's money legs must not be split into unrelated successful commits. If a business process genuinely spans services or multiple steps, document durable reservation/obligation states, parent/step identity, atomic boundaries, and recovery against original committed intent. Each financial step must balance and remain traceable. A pending or partially fulfilled obligation is not 'paid' or 'complete.' Never claim a single ACID transaction spans unrelated services, the whole event lifetime, or the network when it does not.
9. Presentation follows commitment: real-time events, notifications, history, and balances reference the committed operation and revision. Recover an uncertain result by reading/resuming the original operation. No UI, animation, watchdog, or event consumer can create a second financial effect. Preserve the settlement/presentation distinction recorded as SETTLEMENT_COMMITTED versus AWARD_PRESENT in the bomb-pot specification, adapting to verified current names rather than inventing parallel states.
10. Prove conservation at the right scope: reconcile each account from an evidenced opening balance plus immutable postings, and reconcile every disjoint custody/pool boundary in its own unit. Global net zero is insufficient if the wrong player was debited or credited. Prove the correct amount, beneficiary, source, rule, and one-time effect for every tested operation.
11. Commit-time enforcement: prove complete journal groups balance by unit and all authoritative custody, obligation, and business-state invariants hold when the transaction commits. A row-level CHECK cannot by itself enforce a sum across other rows. Cover every permitted caller/role and prevent incomplete or later-appendable committed posting groups. In PostgreSQL, use an appropriate controlled posting/constraint design for the actual version; do not merely assume the isolation level supplies accounting rules. Test actual successful and rejected commits, including deferred validation, not only a test harness that always rolls back. Retry the complete decision transaction after an eligible serialization/deadlock failure using the original business identity; recover uncertain commit status before taking any new effect.
12. Durable owed work: when an accepted commit creates downstream work, that work must be durably discoverable from the same authoritative commit through the existing transactional outbox, committed obligation/state transition, or verified committed-change equivalent. Enqueueing only after commit leaves an unacceptable crash gap. Record consumer deduplication, checkpointing, retry/escalation, and visible unresolved/dead-lettered obligations. Test crash after financial commit before publication and after consumer effect before acknowledgment. Delivery can repeat; financial effects must not.
13. Durability and recovery: document the actual commit-acknowledgment, replication/failover, backup/restore, and obsolete-writer fencing boundary. Verify journal history, operation identities, obligations, and publication/checkpoint state recover consistently. Record the measured recovery point and recovery time objectives and whether acknowledged financial state can be lost in any claimed supported failure scenario. For PostgreSQL, inspect applicable WAL, fsync, synchronous-commit, and replication guarantees rather than infer them from ACID terminology. Do not weaken financial durability for latency. A zero-loss claim requires matching deployment evidence and an isolated recovery rehearsal; any gap against the required guarantee remains a foundation blocker until resolved or explicitly changed by the user. Restored copies must not blindly replay uncertain external effects or manufacture replacement history.
14. Migration integrity: verify both forward upgrades of the actual deployed schema and fresh installation where supported. Preserve immutable history, semantic operation identities, outstanding reservations/tickets/obligations, policy snapshots, and in-flight hand/event ownership across old/new application and worker versions. Backfills require evidenced opening data, dry-run calculations, and replay-safe execution. No cutover may leave two independent financial writers. Specify a compatible rollback or forward repair that cannot resurrect the defect or erase committed financial history.

Required per-project evidence: list every financial entry point and all transitive writers; map it to the canonical atomic operation and journal/obligation records; prove it cannot bypass that path; document each exception as a required finding; and show meaningful real transaction tests for normal behavior, mid-transaction failure, concurrent spends, duplicate delivery, same-key/conflicting-payload reuse, different-request-key attempts against the same nonrepeatable business event, crash/unknown commit, and unauthorized direct writes. Include legitimate distinct and incremental operations so deduplication cannot silently suppress money actually owed. Mocks alone cannot certify atomic database behavior.

G5 defines shared invariant tests and the posting contract. G1-G4 prove their domain routes and units; G6 proves no event reports an uncommitted financial result, duplicate or reordered delivery cannot duplicate a financial effect, and clients deduplicate committed revisions and recover missing results through the documented catch-up contract. G7 proves no retired or retained worker bypasses the atomic path. G8 checks the evidence and refuses completion claims for dependent financial work with unverified gaps. Reuse valid shared evidence, but do not substitute it for missing caller/integration proof.

The target is zero unexplained chip drift and no accepted partial or duplicate financial effect within the defined committed boundaries. Do not promise that finite testing proves future perfection. Apply the three distinct completion gates in Assignment, Workspace, And Phase Controls: an audit phase can finish with fully recorded repairs still planned; delivery phases must close their assigned required work; foundation acceptance must close all required financial gaps. No required route with a bypass, mutable-history repair, unexplained balance, or unverified atomic/durability boundary may pass FOUNDATION_ACCEPTANCE. Keep unrelated valid play available while individual invalid operations fail atomically and management receives an accurate alert.

## Union Layering Mission

Audit and repair the entire hierarchy that connects unions, clubs, owners, managers, super agents, agents, subagents, players, games, wallets, policies, commissions, and reports. Treat union behavior as a first-class system with explicit tenant boundaries. A club-only flow that breaks when a club joins a union is a defect in this project's scope.

G4 owns membership, hierarchy, permissions, configuration precedence, and economic allocation policy. G5 owns ledger and settlement execution. Game projects own game behavior. G6 owns event delivery. Every cross-boundary contract must have one writer and acceptance by its consumers.

### Required Research

Research official PokerBROS, PPPoker, ClubGG, and other relevant club/union platform documentation where it is publicly available. Include club creation/joining, union membership, shared game liquidity, role capabilities, delegated management, wallet/credit boundaries, and reporting. Track sources that describe marketing behavior but do not document custody or internal settlement. Never infer a competitor's financial liability model merely from the phrase 'shared player pool.'

Starter Sources, Reopen And Expand:

- https://pokerbros.net/en/faq
- https://www.pokerbros.net/en/blog/create-your-own-poker-club-on-pokerbros-in-minutes
- https://pokerbros.net/club-promotion-rules.html

Find official sources for the other named platforms during your research. Add primary database/authorization documentation matching the actual stack and versions. Validate behavior with available authorized product evidence; do not sign up, spend money, or obtain privileged access merely to fill a research gap.

### Complete Scope Checklist

1. Map all actual entity types and relations. Verify whether unions may themselves have parent layers, whether clubs can belong to multiple unions, whether users can have multiple club memberships, and whether agent trees are scoped per club or union. Do not assume a single global parent. Record cardinality, lifecycle, stable identity, and source of truth for each relationship.
2. Audit union/club creation, invitations, applications, approvals, admission, membership activation, suspension/leave/removal, migration between unions, ownership transfer, dissolution, and archived entities. Define effective dates and what happens to active seats, registrations, tickets, unsettled obligations, reports, and existing wallet balances when membership changes.
3. Audit every role and capability: owner, union administrator, manager, super agent, agent, subagent, player, service identity, and any actual custom role. Build a resource/action/scope matrix for read, create, update, approve, transfer, invite, configure, report, and administer. Test backend and database authorization, not only hidden buttons.
4. Audit upward/downward visibility, sibling isolation, membership overlap, role promotion/demotion, delegation, revoked invitations, token/session freshness, and privilege revocation on active channels. A player belonging to two clubs must not let one club's agent see the other club's private records. An agent cannot promote themselves, move unrelated players, or modify rates outside their authority.
5. Verify hierarchy integrity: no cycles, no orphaned active memberships, no cross-tenant parent injection, no duplicate active placement where prohibited, no self-parenting, and stable descendant queries. Concurrent reparenting must not bypass these invariants. Historical reports must retain the relationships effective at the transaction time.
6. Audit game sharing and visibility across union/club boundaries: creation ownership, access lists, join eligibility, union-wide versus club-only tables, game filters, seat eligibility, invitations, tournament registration, promotions, waitlists, and returning/reconnecting players. Trace the club that originated the player, the operator of the game, and the accounts that owe or receive settlement.
7. Build the complete configuration inheritance matrix for game permissions, fees/rake, caps, BBJ, backup BBJ, promos, time limits, credit limits, reports, commission/rakeback, and supported custom settings. Define default, inherited, overridden, disabled, null, and explicitly zero as distinct states. Snapshot applicable policy versions at the correct business boundary; a later administrative edit must not rewrite a past hand's economics.
8. Audit all wallet and credit views, transfer requests, approvals, internal transfers, club/union settlement, outstanding receivables/payables, reporting cutoffs, and disputes/corrections. G5 must verify balanced postings and custody. Credit limits and debt are not the same as newly minted chips. Shared game access does not imply a single unrestricted wallet.
9. Recover approved rakeback policy, including previously requested super-agent/agent/subagent/player allocation and minimum upline margin. Prior examples included a 70% super-agent allocation, agents at 40%-60%, subagents at 10%-30%, players at 10%-50%, and a minimum 10% upline margin. Treat these as policy context to verify, not universal industry rules or permission to reset live rates. Establish whether margin means percentage points of eligible rake or another basis from authoritative requirements. If unresolved, isolate that policy decision rather than inventing an interpretation.
10. Prove commission allocations cannot exceed the distributable base. Define gross versus net percentages, who funds player rakeback, retained upline margin, effective-time changes, reparenting, reversals, and deterministic rounding. Examples must include a super agent with both direct players and subordinate agents, an agent with direct players and subagents, and different per-player entitlements. Do not apply a series of percentages blindly or double-credit the same rake across layers.
11. Audit club/union leaderboards, player counts, wallets, agent statements, settlements, rake/rakeback, BBJ, promos, transfers, balances, exports, and audit trails. Define attribution for a player active in several clubs. Compare the same reporting period and unit; do not hide differences with a presentation-only total.
12. Verify all UI flows on desktop/mobile, including expanding 'My Wallets' to show every wallet attached to the current user's relevant club/union context, not an assumed maximum of three. Preserve approved design assets, owner messaging, active-table state, and context switching. Ensure management notification routing resolves the correct authorized recipients without leaking another tenant's data.
13. Audit membership APIs, database functions, triggers, policies, cached role claims, materialized hierarchy paths, background jobs, support/admin tooling, and legacy routes. An alternate endpoint must not bypass the rules enforced by the new UI.
14. Trace every hierarchy/configuration/permission dependency that can block authorized game creation, entry, activation, continuation, or closeout. Fix defective denials and stale inheritance while preserving legitimate restrictions. Membership or role changes during a game must retain the correct beneficiary, obligation, history access policy, and approved settlement authority; they cannot orphan already owed funds or route payment to a fallback wallet. Supply bounded foundation design/review acceptance when G8 assigns it, without waiting for this full project to launch.

### Mandatory Acceptance Scenarios

Use a synthetic hierarchy with at least two unions, multiple clubs, a club outside those unions, sibling agents, multiple levels, and users with overlapping memberships. Verify permitted cross-club game access and forbidden cross-tenant data access separately. Exercise concurrent reparenting, demotion during an open session, membership removal during play, union migration with outstanding obligations, simultaneous transfer approval, inherited zero values, and policy edits during an active hand/event.

For each financial allocation case, produce a trace from gross qualifying rake through every layer to the final beneficiary and retained margins, accepted by G5. Verify corrections and attribution before/after effective dates. Confirm all corresponding UI/report/event paths reflect the same scoped result.

### Phases And Exit

Plan from actual dependencies, typically entity/role integrity, policy inheritance, union game access, wallet/settlement contracts, and reports/live UX. Keep financial primitives with G5. Exit requires every hierarchy lifecycle and scoped flow accounted for, complete authorization evidence, conserved allocation contracts, no duplicate ownership, and verified delivery. First check the foundation gate and perform only its permitted assignments. After G8 accepts the foundation, launch your independent research/audit Phase 1 swarm under this complete prompt.
